\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}
are defined in Appendix
S6.2
.
Note that

\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{H}}$}+\stk(\boldsymbol{0}_{N\times N\times p},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}).

(S14)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})^{2}\|\boldsymbol{G}_{i}^{I*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\left\|(\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{G}^{II,1*}_{k}-\frac{\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k}^{*}}{\gamma_{k}^{*}}\boldsymbol{G}^{II,2*}_{k}\right\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\left\|(\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{G}^{II,2*}_{k}+\frac{\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k}^{*}}{\gamma_{k}^{*}}\boldsymbol{G}^{II,1*}_{k}\right\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})^{2}\|\boldsymbol{G}_{i}^{I*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{s}(\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*})^{2}(\|\boldsymbol{G}^{II,1*}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{G}^{II,2*}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\frac{(\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k}^{*})^{2}}{\gamma_{k}^{*2}}(\|\boldsymbol{G}^{II,1*}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{G}^{II,2*}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}),

(S15)

which, together with Assumption
3
(iii), leads to

\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}.

(S16)

By the simple inequalities
(|x|+|y|)/2\leq\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}\leq|x|+|y|
, we have

0.5(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}})\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
,
and thus in view of (
S16
) we further have

0.5\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right)\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}},

(S17)

where
\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
.
By Lemma
S.2
,
\sigma_{\min,L}=\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}))>0
.
Then it follows from (
S17
) that

0.5\sigma_{\min,L}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right)\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{H}}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sigma_{\max,L}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}\right).

Combining this with (
S10
), (
S14
), (
S16
), as well as the fact that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{\textrm{MA}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{\textrm{MA}*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{H}}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\sigma_{\max,L}+\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{L}}{1-\bar{\rho}}\left(\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}+\frac{\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}}{2}\right)\right\}\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{\max,L}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}+\frac{C_{L}}{1-\bar{\rho}}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right)\alpha

and

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{H}}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\{0.5\sigma_{\min,L}-\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{L}}{1-\bar{\rho}}\left(\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}+\frac{\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}}{2}\right)\right\}\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\left(\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\sigma_{\min,L}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{C_{L}}{1-\bar{\rho}}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right)\alpha.

Thus, by taking

\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}=\min\left\{2,\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}(1-\bar{\rho})\sigma_{\min,L}}{8\sqrt{2}C_{L}}\right\},

we can show that

c_{\Delta}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right)\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\Delta}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right),

where

c_{\Delta}=c_{l}\cdot\sigma_{\min,L}\asymp 1\quad\text{and}\quad C_{\Delta}=c_{u}\cdot\max\left\{\sigma_{\max,L},(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}\right\}\asymp 1,

with
c_{l}=0.25\min\{1,\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\}
and
c_{u}=1+\sqrt{2}(\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*})^{-1}+(4\sqrt{2}+2)C_{L}
. By Lemma
S.2
, we have
c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\asymp 1
,
c_{\Delta}\asymp 1
, and
C_{\Delta}\asymp 1
. The proof of this lemma is complete.

S6.4
Proof of Theorem
3

Let
\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{y}_{t-2}^{\prime},\dots)^{\prime}
.
Denote by
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}
with
j\geq 1
the frontal slices of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
, i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}=(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\dots)
. Denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}&S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j},\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle,\\
&S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j},\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle,\\
&S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\rangle.\end{split}

(S18)

The following three lemmas are sufficient for the proof of
Theorem
3
.

Lemma S.3

(Strong convexity and smoothness properties)
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, then with probability at least
1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-3e^{-cN}
,

\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}.

Lemma S.4

(Deviation bound)
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
S.3
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-5e^{-cN}
,

\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}.

Lemma S.5

(Effects of initial values)
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})d_{\pazocal{R}}
, then with probability at least
1-\{2+\sqrt{\kappa_{2}/\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})}\}\sqrt{N/\{(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})T\}}
,

|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\lesssim\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2},\quad|S_{i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},\quad i=2,3,\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}.

Now we prove Theorem
3
.
Note that
\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}
.
Due to the optimality of
\mathscr{\widehat{A}}
, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2},

Then, since
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}+\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\rangle+\underbrace{\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\rangle}_{S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle+S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})-S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}),

(S19)

where
S_{2}(\cdot)
and
S_{3}(\cdot)
are defined as in (
S18
),
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
.

Moreover, applying the inequality
\|\boldsymbol{a}-\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\rangle
with
\boldsymbol{a}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}
and
\boldsymbol{b}=\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
, we can lower bound the left-hand side of (
S19
) to further obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle+S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})-S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}),

(S20)

where
S_{1}(\cdot)
is defined as in (
S18
).
It is worth pointing out that
S_{i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
for
1\leq i\leq 3
capture the initialization effect of
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}=\boldsymbol{0}
for
s\leq 0
on the estimation.

Note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
and
\kappa_{2}\geq\kappa_{1}
. Suppose that the high probability events in Lemmas
S.3
–
S.5
hold. Then we can derive the estimation error bound from (
S20
):

\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},\quad\text{or}\quad\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{\kappa_{1}^{2}T}}.

Furthermore, applying Lemma
S.5
again, we can derive the prediction error bound from (
S19
) and the above result as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{\kappa_{1}T}.

The proof of this theorem is complete.

S6.5
Proof of Lemma
S.1

Proof of (i):
By definition,
\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{i})=\lambda_{i}^{j}
for
1\leq i\leq r
, and
\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=\gamma_{k}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{k})
and
\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=\gamma_{k}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{k})
for
1\leq k\leq s
.
Then the first-order derivatives are
\nabla\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{i})=j\lambda_{i}^{j-1}
,
\nabla_{\gamma}\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j\gamma_{k}^{j-1}\cos(j\theta_{k})
,

\nabla_{\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=-j\gamma_{k}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{k})
,
\nabla_{\gamma}\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j\gamma_{k}^{j-1}\sin(j\theta_{k})
, and

\nabla_{\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j\gamma_{k}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{k})
. The second-order derivatives are

\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{i})=j(j-1)\lambda_{i}^{j-2}
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j(j-1)\gamma_{k}^{j-2}\cos(j\theta_{k})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=-j^{2}\gamma_{k}^{j-1}\sin(j\theta_{k})
,
\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=-j^{2}\gamma_{k}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{k})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j(j-1)\gamma_{k}^{j-2}\sin(j\theta_{k})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=j^{2}\gamma_{k}^{j-1}\cos(j\theta_{k})
, and
\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\ell_{j}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})=-j^{2}\gamma_{k}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{k})
. By Assumption
3
(i), there exists
\rho_{1}>0
such that
\max\{|\lambda_{1}|,\ldots,|\lambda_{r}|,\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{s}\}\leq\rho_{1}<\bar{\rho}
. Thus,

\max_{1\leq i\leq r,1\leq k\leq s,1\leq h\leq 2}\{|\nabla\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{i})|,\|\nabla\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})\|_{2},|\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{i})|,\|\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\}\leq C_{L}\bar{\rho}^{j}.

by choosing
C_{L}
dependent on
\rho_{1}
and
\bar{\rho}
such that
C_{L}\geq 2j^{2}(\rho_{1}/\bar{\rho})^{j-2}\bar{\rho}^{-2}
for all
j\geq 1
. Note that
C_{L}
exists and is an absolute constant.

Proof of (ii):

By Assumption
3
,

\max\{|\lambda_{1}^{*}|,\ldots,|\lambda_{r}^{*}|,\gamma_{1}^{*},\ldots,\gamma_{s}^{*}\}\leq\bar{\rho}
, and there exists an absolute constant
C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}>0
such that

\|\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}
for all
1\leq k\leq p
. Then, by a method similar to (
S18
),
we can show that

\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\|\boldsymbol{G}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}
for
1\leq j\leq p
, and

\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}|\ell_{j,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})|\|\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\bar{\rho}^{j-p}\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\|\boldsymbol{G}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\bar{\rho}^{j-p}(r+2s)C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}

for
j\geq p+1
.
Then, taking
C_{*}=\bar{\rho}^{-p}\max\{(r+2s)C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}},1\}
, we accomplish the proof of (ii).

S6.6
Proof of Lemma
S.2

Let
J=2(r+2s)
. Consider the following partitions of the
\infty\times(p+J)
matrix
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
:

\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\boldsymbol{I}_{p}&\\
&\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{I}_{p}&\\
&\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\\
&\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\end{matrix}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})=\left(\boldsymbol{L}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}),\boldsymbol{L}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta}),\nabla\boldsymbol{L}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}),\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{L}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)
is further partitioned into two blocks, the
J\times J
block

\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
and the
\infty\times J
remainder block
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
. Note that for
1\leq j\leq J
, the
j
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is

\boldsymbol{L}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\omega}):=\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\right)^{\prime},\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)^{\prime},\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\right)^{\prime},\left(\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)^{\prime}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=(\lambda_{1}^{j},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{j})^{\prime}
,
\nabla\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=(j\lambda_{1}^{j-1},\dots,j\lambda_{r}^{j-1})^{\prime}
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{1}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{1}),\gamma_{1}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{1}),\dots,\gamma_{s}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{s}),\gamma_{s}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{s})\right)^{\prime},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\left(-j\gamma_{1}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{1}),j\gamma_{1}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{1}),\dots,-j\gamma_{s}^{j}\sin(j\theta_{s}),j\gamma_{s}^{j}\cos(j\theta_{s})\right)^{\prime}.

For
j\geq 1
, the
j
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is
\boldsymbol{L}_{J+j}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
.

By Lemma
S.1
(i), we have
\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{J\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}C_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2j}}\leq C_{L}\sqrt{J}\bar{\rho}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}=C_{\bar{\rho}}
. Then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\leq\max\left\{1,\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\}\leq\max\left\{1,\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right\}\leq\max\{1,C_{\bar{\rho}}\}

(S21)

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\leq\|\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\bar{\rho}}.

(S22)

It remains to derive a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
. To this end, we first derive a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
by lower bounding the determinant of
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
.
For any
(\gamma,\theta)\in[0,1)\times(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
, it can be verified that

\left(\gamma^{j}\cos(j\theta),\gamma^{j}\sin(j\theta)\right)\underbrace{\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
i&-i\end{matrix}\right)}_{:=\boldsymbol{C}_{1}}=\left((\gamma e^{i\theta})^{j},(\gamma e^{-i\theta})^{j}\right)

and

\left(-j\gamma^{j}\sin(j\theta),j\gamma^{j}\cos(j\theta)\right)\underbrace{\left(\begin{matrix}-i&i\\
1&1\end{matrix}\right)}_{:=\boldsymbol{C}_{2}}=\left(j(\gamma e^{i\theta})^{j},j(\gamma e^{-i\theta})^{j}\right).

Let
\boldsymbol{P}_{1}=\diag(\boldsymbol{I}_{r},\boldsymbol{C}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{C}_{1},\boldsymbol{I}_{r},\boldsymbol{C}_{2},\dots,\boldsymbol{C}_{2})
be a
J\times J
block diagonal matrix consisting of two identity matrices
\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
and
s
repeated blocks of
\boldsymbol{C}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{C}_{2}
.
We then have
\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{1})=(-2i)^{2s}=4^{s}
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\boldsymbol{P}_{1}=\left(\begin{matrix}x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&2x_{1}^{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&2x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J}&x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J}&Jx_{1}^{J}&Jx_{2}^{J}&\cdots&Jx_{r+2s}^{J}\end{matrix}\right):=\boldsymbol{P}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{J\times J},

where
x_{i}=\lambda_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq r
, while
x_{r+2k-1}=\gamma_{k}e^{i\theta_{k}}
and
x_{r+2k}=\gamma_{k}e^{-i\theta_{k}}
for
1\leq k\leq s
, and
i
is the imaginary unit.

We subtract the
j
th column of
\boldsymbol{P}_{2}
from its
(r+2s+j)
th column, for all
1\leq j\leq r+2s
, and obtain a matrix with the same determinant as
\boldsymbol{P}_{2}
as follows,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}_{3}=\left(\begin{matrix}x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&0&0&\cdots&0\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J}&x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J}&(J-1)x_{1}^{J}&(J-1)x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&(J-1)x_{r+2s}^{J}\end{matrix}\right).

Note that
\boldsymbol{P}_{3}=\boldsymbol{P}_{4}\boldsymbol{P}_{5}
, where

\boldsymbol{P}_{4}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&\cdots&1&0&0&\cdots&0\\
x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&2x_{1}^{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&2x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J-1}&x_{2}^{J-1}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J-1}&(J-1)x_{1}^{J-1}&(J-1)x_{2}^{J-1}&\cdots&(J-1)x_{r+2s}^{J-1}\end{matrix}\right)

is a generalized Vandermonde matrix
(
Li and Tan,, 2008
)
, and
\boldsymbol{P}_{5}=\diag\{x_{1},\dots,x_{r+2s},x_{1},\dots,x_{r+2s}\}
. By
Li and Tan, (2008)
,

|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{4})|=\prod_{i=1}^{r+2s}x_{i}\prod_{1\leq k<j\leq r+2s}(x_{j}-x_{k})^{4}
.
As a result,

|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{2})|=|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{3})|=|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{4})||\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{5})|=\prod_{j=1}^{r+2s}|x_{j}|^{3}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq r+2s}(x_{j}-x_{k})^{4}\geq\nu_{1}^{3J/2}\nu_{2}^{J(J/2-1)},

where
\nu_{1}=\min\{|x_{k}|,1\leq k\leq r+2s\}
,
\nu_{2}=\min\{|x_{j}-x_{k}|,1\leq j<k\leq r+2s\}
, and
J=2(r+2s)
. It follows that

\displaystyle|\det(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))|=\frac{|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{2})|}{|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{1})|}\geq 0.25^{s}\nu_{1}^{3J/2}\nu_{2}^{J(J/2-1)}>0,

(S23)

and hence
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is full-rank. Moreover, combining (
S22
) and (
S23
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\geq\frac{|\det(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))|}{\sigma_{\max}^{J-1}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))}\geq\frac{0.25^{s}\nu_{1}^{3J/2}\nu_{2}^{J(J/2-1)}}{C_{\bar{\rho}}^{J-1}}.

(S24)

Note that the right side of (
S24
) when
\boldsymbol{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}
is
c_{\bar{\rho}}
, and Assumption
3
(ii) ensures that
c_{\bar{\rho}}>0
is an absolute constant.

Finally, similar to (
S21
), by the Courant–Fischer theorem, it can be shown that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\geq\min\left\{1,\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\}\geq\min\left\{1,\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\},

which, together with (
S24
), leads to a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
. Applying this and the upper bound in (
S21
) to
\boldsymbol{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}
under Assumption
3
(ii), we accomplish the proof of this lemma.

S6.7
Proof of Lemma
S.3

It suffices to show that the event stated in this lemma holds uniformly over the intersection of
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
and the sphere
\pazocal{S}(\delta):=\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times\infty}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta\}
, for some radius
\delta>0
to be chosen such that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)\neq\emptyset
, since the event will remain true if we multiply
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
by any nonzero real number.

Recall that any
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
can be written as
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{3}\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}:=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$})
,
for some
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times d}
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}
dependent on
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
with
\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
.
In addition, by (
S13
) and (
S14
), we can further write

\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\times_{3}\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})+\stk(\boldsymbol{0}_{N\times N\times p},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}).

Note that throughout the proofs of Lemmas
S.3
–
S.5
, we will suppress the dependence of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
and
\mathscr{R}
on
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.
Thus,

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\times_{3}\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}=(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p},

where

\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{y}_{t-2}^{\prime},\dots)^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}.

(S25)

For simplicity, denote
\boldsymbol{X}=(\boldsymbol{x}_{T},\dots,\boldsymbol{x}_{1})
,
\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}=(\boldsymbol{x}_{T-p},\dots,\boldsymbol{x}_{1-p})
and
\boldsymbol{Z}=(\boldsymbol{z}_{T},\dots,\boldsymbol{z}_{1})
. Then
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{X}=(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}
, and hence

\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
,
i.e.,

\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\geq\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

(S26)

and

\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(S27)

Now we restrict our attention to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, where
\delta>0
will be specified later. If
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, since
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta
, then it follows from Lemma
1
that

\delta C_{\Delta}^{-1}\leq\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\delta C_{\Delta}^{-1}}{\alpha}\leq\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq\frac{\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}}{\alpha},

(S28)

where
\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}=\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}
and
\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
.
To guarantee that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)\neq\emptyset
, it is sufficient to choose
\delta>0
such that

\frac{\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}}{\alpha}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}.

(S29)

Furthermore, by (
S17
) and (
S28
), we can obtain the following bounds of
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
by restricting the corresponding
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}&\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}\right)\asymp\delta,\\
\inf_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}&\geq 0.5\delta C_{\Delta}^{-1}(1+\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}})\asymp\delta.\end{split}

(S30)

Next we establish the following union bounds that hold for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
:

(i)

If
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta):\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{1}}{8}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

(ii)

if
T\gtrsim N
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\right\}\geq 1-3e^{-N\log 9}.

Proof of (i):
Note that by (
S30
), for every
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, its corresponding
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
has a bounded Frobenius norm in the order of
\delta
. This relationship between
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
allows us to convert the problem of finding union bounds over all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
to that over all
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
with Frobenius norm in the order of
\delta
.

Moreover, note that for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
, it holds
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Xi}
is defined as in (
S5
), and hence

\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}}{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mid\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\}.

(S31)

Then, by taking
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}/\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
, it follows directly from (
S44
) in Lemma
S.6
that, if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}:\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{8}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

Thus, by restricting our attention to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
and combining (
S30
) with the above result, we can obtain (i).

Proof of (ii):
First note that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}
, where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}=\stk(\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2},\dots)
.
For all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, it follows from (
S28
) and the choice of
\delta
in (
S29
) that

\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1},\quad\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1},\quad\text{and}\quad\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}.

(S32)

Combining the above bounds with (
S10
), we have

\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{R}_{1j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{R}_{2j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{R}_{3j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}},

(S33)

where

C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}=C_{L}c_{\Delta}^{-1}(\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{2}c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}/2+1)\asymp 1
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}&=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-i}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{R}_{i}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\\
&=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-i},\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
&=\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right\}^{2}.\end{split}

(S34)

In addition, we can show that

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}=\trace\left\{\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{R}_{j}^{\prime}\right\}\leq\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\right\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}.

As a result,

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\right\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}^{1/2}\right)^{2}.

(S35)

Then, by (
S33
), (
S35
) and (
S46
) in Lemma
S.7
, if
T\gtrsim N
, with probability at least
1-3e^{-N\log 9}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{j\sigma^{2}+1}\right)^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}),

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{j\sigma^{2}+1}\asymp 1
. Thus (ii) is verified.

Finally, it remains to combine (
S26
) and (
S27
) with the results in (i) and (ii).
To ensure that the lower bound of
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
in (i) dominates the upper bound of
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}
in (ii), we only need
\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\lesssim\sqrt{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}/\{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\}}
. In view of (
S28
), this can be guaranteed by choosing
\delta>0
such that

\frac{\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}}{\alpha}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})}}.

(S36)

Combining the above condition with (
S29
), we can obtain the desirable
\delta
. Then we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta):c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-2e^{-N\log 9}.

Since
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}
and
C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}
are absolute constants, and
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta
for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, the proof of this lemma is complete.

S6.8
Proof of Lemma
S.4

Similar to Lemma
S.3
, it suffices to prove that the result of Lemma
S.4
holds uniformly over the intersection of
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
and the sphere
\pazocal{S}(\delta):=\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times\infty}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta\}
,
where the radius
\delta>0
is chosen to satisfy condition (
S29
) to ensure that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)\neq\emptyset
. Specifically, we will prove that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-5e^{-cN}.

(S37)

From the proof of Lemma
S.3
,
for every
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
, the corresponding
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}=\stk(\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2},\dots)
satisfy

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\lesssim\delta,\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}}{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1},\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\quad\text{for all }j\geq 1;

(S38)

see (
S29
)–(
S33
).
Moreover, by the definition of
\boldsymbol{R}_{j}
in (
S9
), it can be verified that, for all
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{colsp}(\boldsymbol{R}_{j})\subseteq\mathrm{colsp}(\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(1)},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(1)}^{*}\})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathrm{colsp}(\boldsymbol{R}_{j}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{colsp}(\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(2)},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(2)}^{*}\}),

where
\mathrm{colsp}(\boldsymbol{U})
represents the column space of a matrix
\boldsymbol{U}
, and
\mathrm{colsp}(\{\boldsymbol{U},\boldsymbol{V}\})
represents the span of all column vectors of the matrices
\boldsymbol{U}
and
\boldsymbol{V}
. Since for any
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
it holds

\textrm{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)})\leq\pazocal{R}_{i}
and
\textrm{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}^{*})\leq\pazocal{R}_{i}
for
i=1,2
, we then have

\textrm{rank}(\boldsymbol{R}_{j})\leq 2(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}):=2\pazocal{R}_{\wedge},\quad\text{for all }j\geq 1.

(S39)

Note that

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}
. As a result, by (
S38
) and (
S39
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle+\delta\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(2\pazocal{R}_{\wedge})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle.

Applying (
S82
) and (
S47
) in Lemmas
S.6
and
S.7
, respectively, we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}+\delta\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\frac{2\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N\pazocal{R}_{\wedge}}{T}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}(2j\sigma^{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}

with probability at least
1-e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}}-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-4e^{-N\log 9}
, where the second inequality follows from the fact that
d_{\pazocal{R}}\gtrsim N\pazocal{R}_{\wedge}
,
\kappa_{2}\gtrsim\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}(2j\sigma^{2}+1)\asymp 1
. Since
e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}}\leq e^{-cN}
, (
S37
) holds and the proof of this lemma is complete.

S6.9
Proof of Lemma
S.5

Similar to Lemmas
S.3
and
S.3
, it suffices to prove that the result of Lemma
S.5
holds uniformly over the intersection of
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
and the sphere
\pazocal{S}(\delta):=\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times\infty}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta\}
,
where the radius
\delta>0
is chosen to satisfy condition (
S29
) to ensure that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)\neq\emptyset
.

Recall from the proof of Lemma
S.3
that for every
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
,

\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1},\quad\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1},\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j},

where
\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}=\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}
and
\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
; see (
S32
) and (
S33
). Combining this with (
S6
), (
S7
), Lemma
S.1
(i) and Assumption
3
(iii), we can show that

\|\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\ell}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\ell}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}(1+C_{L})\sqrt{r+2s}\bar{\rho}^{j},

where
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
represents the
j
th row of the matrix
\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})=\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{I}(\lambda_{1}),\dots,\boldsymbol{\ell}^{I}(\lambda_{r}),\boldsymbol{\ell}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{1}),\dots,\boldsymbol{\ell}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{s})\right)
, and further that

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j+p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta\left\{c_{\Delta}^{-1}(1+C_{L})\sqrt{r+2s}+c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}\right\}\bar{\rho}^{j},\quad j\geq 1.

Note that
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\boldsymbol{G}_{j}-\boldsymbol{G}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}
for
1\leq j\leq p
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\delta C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j},\quad\text{for all }j\geq 1,

(S40)

where
C_{1}=\left\{c_{\Delta}^{-1}(1+C_{L})\sqrt{r+2s}+c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{R}$}}\right\}\bar{\rho}^{-p}\asymp 1
.

In addition, by Lemma
S.11
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})=\trace(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq N\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq N\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}).

Combining this with Lemma
S.1
(ii) and (
S40
), for all
j\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2})\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}^{2})\right\}^{1/2}\leq C_{*}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N}

(S41)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}\right)\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}^{1/2}\leq\delta C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N}.

(S42)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
S42
),

\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{j+k}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N\leq\frac{\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{2}N}{T},

where
C_{2}=2C_{1}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{3}\asymp 1
. Similarly, by (
S41
) and (
S42
),

\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\delta C_{*}C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j+k}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N\leq\frac{\delta C_{3}\kappa_{2}N}{T},

where
C_{3}=2C_{*}C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{2}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{3}\asymp 1
. Moreover, note that
\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2})\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})}\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})N}
. Then by (
S42
) and a method similar to the above,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\delta C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})}N\leq\frac{\delta C_{4}\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})}N}{T},

where
C_{4}=2C_{1}\bar{\rho}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{2}\asymp 1
.
By Markov’s inequality, we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{1}\right\}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\}}{\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{1}}\leq\frac{\kappa_{2}N}{\kappa_{1}T}\leq\sqrt{\frac{N}{(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})T}},

(S43)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}N^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})Td_{\pazocal{R}}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}N}{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})T}},

and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{N^{2}}{Td_{\pazocal{R}}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{N}{(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})T}},

where the last inequality in (
S43
) uses the condition that
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})d_{\pazocal{R}}
, and
d_{\pazocal{R}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})N
.
Then the sum of the above three tail probabilities is
(2+\sqrt{\kappa_{2}/\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})})\sqrt{N/\{(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})T\}}
.
Thus, we have proved the result of this lemma for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
. Replacing
\delta
by
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
in the above inequalities, we accomplish the proof for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
.

S6.10
Auxiliary lemmas for the proofs of Lemmas
S.3
and
S.4

Lemmas
S.3
and
S.4
are established based on the auxiliary results below. In particular, Lemmas
S.6
and
S.7
are both used directly in the proofs of Lemmas
S.3
and
S.4
. The covering and discretization results in Lemma
S.8
play an essential role in the proof of Lemma
S.6
.
The last three lemmas are useful results for the proofs of both Lemmas
S.6
and
S.7
:
Lemmas
S.9
and
S.10
give high-probabilitity concentration and Hanson-Wright inequalities for stationary time series, respectively; and Lemma
S.11
provides deterministic bounds for covariance matrices of stationary time series. As in (
S31
), let
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mid\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\}
. Note that the following definition is used in Lemma
S.8
.

Definition 1

(Generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
)
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, we say that

\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is a generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
if
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Xi}
, and for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
such that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\epsilon
. However,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is not required to be a subset of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
; that is,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
may not be an
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.

Lemma S.6
.

Suppose that Assumptions
3
and
2
hold and
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
. Let
\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}
be defined as in (
S25
). Then

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{8}\leq\inf_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

(S44)

and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}}{T}}\right\}\geq 1-e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}}-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

(S45)

Lemma S.7
.

Suppose that Assumptions
3
and
2
hold and
T\gtrsim N
. Then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall j\geq 1:\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\geq 1-3e^{-N\log 9}

(S46)

and moreover, if
T\gtrsim N\pazocal{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall j\geq 1:\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\leq 24\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})(2j\sigma^{2}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})N\pazocal{R}}{T}}\right\}\geq 1-4e^{-N\pazocal{R}\log 9}.

(S47)

Lemma S.8

(Covering number and discretization for
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<2/3
, let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
be a minimal generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.

(i)

The cardinality of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
satisfies

\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)|\lesssim d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(1/\epsilon),

where
d_{\pazocal{R}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})N
.

(ii)

There exist absolute constants
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}},C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}>0
independent of
\epsilon
such that for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
, it holds
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}
.

(iii)

For any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(d+r+2s)}
and
\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(d+r+2s)\times T}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(1-1.5\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle,

(S48)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-1.5\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

(S49)

In Lemmas
S.9
–
S.11
below, we adopt notations as follows. Let
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
be a time series taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{M}
,
where
M
is an arbitrary positive integer. If
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is stationary with mean zero, then we denote the covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
by

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}^{\prime})
.
In addition, let
\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{w}_{T}^{\prime},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}_{1}^{\prime})^{\prime}
, and denote its covariance matrix by

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}^{\prime})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\boldsymbol{w}_{t-\ell}^{\prime})
is the lag-
\ell
autocovariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(0)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}
.
For the time series
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
, accordingly we define
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t}^{\prime})
and
\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}^{\prime})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T}
, where
\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{T}^{\prime},\dots,\boldsymbol{y}_{1}^{\prime})^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-\ell}^{\prime})
is the lag-
\ell
covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(0)
.

Lemma S.9

(Concentration bound for stationary time series)
.

Suppose that Assumption
2
holds for
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
, and
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series. Assume that
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
is
\mathscr{F}_{t-1}
-measurable, where

\mathscr{F}_{t}=\sigma\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-1},\dots\}
for
t\in\mathbb{Z}
is a filtration. Then, for any
a,b>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq a,\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\rVert^{2}\leq b\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})b}\right\}.

Lemma S.10

(Hanson-Wright inequalities for stationary time series)
.

Suppose that Assumption
2
holds for
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
, and
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
has the vector MA(
\infty
) representation

\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-j},

where

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
for all
j
, and
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
. Let
T_{0}
be a fixed integer.

(i)

Then,
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series, and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\frac{M}{\sqrt{T}}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\right\}\leq 2e^{-cM}.

(ii)

For any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{Q\times M}
with
Q\geq 1
and any
\delta>0
, it holds

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\delta\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c\min(\delta,\delta^{2})T}.

Lemma S.11

(Deterministic bounds for covariance matrices of stationary time series)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
3
and
2
hold, and the vector MA(
\infty
) representation of
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
is
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)=\boldsymbol{I}_{N}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{*}B^{j}
.
Let

\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})=\min_{|z|=1}\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z))
and
\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})=\max_{|z|=1}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z))
, where
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z)
is the conjugate transpose of
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)
.

(i)

It holds

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})

and

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}).

(ii)

Define the time series
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
by

\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-i}
,
where
\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{y}_{t-2}^{\prime},\dots)^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{W}=(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\boldsymbol{W}_{2},\dots)\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times\infty}
, and
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
’s are
M\times N
blocks such that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
. Then,
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series. Moreover,

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{W})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{W})

(S50)

and

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\right)^{2}.

(S51)

S6.11
Proof of Lemma
S.6

Proof of (
S44
):

Denote
\boldsymbol{W}=\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
, and let
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
be the
j
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
for
j\geq 1
. Then

\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}
and
\boldsymbol{W}=(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\boldsymbol{W}_{2},\dots)
,
where
\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{y}_{t-2}^{\prime},\dots)^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{W}_{j}=\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
for
j\geq 1
. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})

and Lemma
S.1
(i), we have

\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}=1\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq p\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq C_{L}\sqrt{J}\bar{\rho}^{j}\quad\text{for }j\geq p+1,

where
J=2(r+2s)
. Thus,

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq C_{\bar{\rho}},

where
C_{\bar{\rho}}=C_{L}\sqrt{J}\bar{\rho}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}
. In addition, by Lemma
S.2
,

\min\{1,c_{\bar{\rho}}\}\leq\sigma_{\min,L}=\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{W})\leq\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{W})=\sigma_{\max,L}\leq\max\{1,C_{\bar{\rho}}\}.

Then, by setting
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
, it follows from Lemma
S.11
(ii) that

\kappa_{1}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\kappa_{2}

(S52)

and

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})C_{\bar{\rho}}^{2}\leq\kappa_{2},

(S53)

where
\kappa_{1}=\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\min\{1,c_{\bar{\rho}}^{2}\}
and
\kappa_{2}=\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\max\{1,C_{\bar{\rho}}^{2}\}
.

Furthermore, since
\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
is a zero-mean and stationary time series, where
\mathscr{W}(B)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}B^{i}
, we can apply Lemma
S.10
(ii) with
T_{0}=0
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
, and
\delta=\kappa_{1}/(2\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2})
, in conjunction with (
S52
) and (
S53
),
to obtain

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq 0.5\kappa_{1}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c_{\sigma}(\kappa_{1}/\kappa_{2})^{2}T},

where
c_{\sigma}=c\min\{0.5\sigma^{-2},0.25\sigma^{-4}\}
.
Note that by (
S52
) and
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
, we can show that

\kappa_{1}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\kappa_{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}.

As a result, for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(0.5\kappa_{1}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1.5\kappa_{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-c_{\sigma}(\kappa_{1}/\kappa_{2})^{2}T}.

(S54)

Next we strengthen (
S54
) to union bounds that hold for all
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
. For simplicity, denote
\boldsymbol{Z}=(\boldsymbol{z}_{T},\dots,\boldsymbol{z}_{1})
, and then

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}.

We consider a minimal generalized
\epsilon_{0}
-net
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, where
0<\epsilon_{0}<2/3
will be chosen later. By Lemma
S.8
(ii), any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
satisfies
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}
. Define the event

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})=\left\{\forall\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0}):\sqrt{0.5c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}<\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}<\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}\right\}.

Then, by the pointwise bounds in (
S54
) and the covering number in Lemma
S.8
(i),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{{\mathscr{E}}^{\complement}(\epsilon_{0})\}

\displaystyle\leq e^{Cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})}\mathbb{P}\left[{\left\{0.5c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}\leq\frac{1}{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq 1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right\}}^{\complement}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-c_{\sigma}(\kappa_{1}/\kappa_{2})^{2}T+Cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})\right\}.

(S55)

By Lemma
S.8
(iii), it holds

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}\right\}\subset\left\{\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}}{1-1.5\epsilon_{0}}\right\}.

(S56)

Moreover, for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
and its corresponding
\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
defined as in the proof of Lemma
S.8
(iii), similarly to (
S63
), we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-1.5\epsilon_{0}\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

Taking the infimum over all
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
and combining the result with (
S56
), we can show that on the event
\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})
, it holds

\displaystyle\inf_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{0.5c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}-1.5\epsilon_{0}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}}{1-1.5\epsilon_{0}}\geq\sqrt{0.5c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}-3\epsilon_{0}\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}

if
0<\epsilon_{0}\leq 1/3
. Thus, by setting

\epsilon_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{6}\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{3C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}},\frac{1}{3}\right\},

we have

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\inf_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{0.5c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}}{2}\right\}.

(S57)

As a result, with the above choice of
\epsilon_{0}
, we have

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{8}\leq\inf_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right\},

which, together with (
S55
) and the condition that
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, leads to (
S44
).

Proof of (
S82
):
Consider a minimal generalized
1/3
-net
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)
of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.
By (
S55
) and (
S56
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}>\sqrt{1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}}\right\}\leq 2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})},

under the condition that
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
. Note that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle=\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}^{\prime}\rangle
. Then by Lemma
S.8
, for any
K>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq K\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{2},\;\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right\}+2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq e^{Cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log 3}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(1/3)}\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{2},\;\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right\}+2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})},

where the first inequality follows from (
S48
), and the last from the covering number in Lemma
S.8
(i). For any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}
, we can apply Lemma
S.9
with
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
to obtain the following pointwise bound:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{2},\;\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1.5C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{K^{2}T}{12\sigma^{2}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})}\right\}.

Then, choosing

K
such that
{K^{2}T}/\{12\sigma^{2}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\}\gtrsim d_{\pazocal{R}}
, i.e.,

K\asymp\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{R}}/T}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq K\right\}\leq e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}}+2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{R}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})},

and hence (
S82
). The proof of Lemma
S.6
is complete.

S6.12
Proof of Lemma
S.7

The following result for low-rank matrices is used in the proof of Lemma
S.7
.

Lemma S.12

(Covering number and discretization for low-rank matrices)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})=\{\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}\mid\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1,\rank(\boldsymbol{M})\leq\pazocal{R}\}
, and let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})
be a minimal
1/2
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})
in the Frobenius norm. Then the cardinality of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})
satisfies

\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})|\leq(2N+1)\pazocal{R}\log 18.

Moreover, for any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
, it holds

\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle\leq 4\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle.

Proof of Lemma
S.12
.

The covering number is given by Lemma 3.1 in
Candes and Plan, (2011)
.
For any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})
, there exists
\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})
satisfying
\|\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq 1/2
. Note that the rank of
\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}
is at most
2\pazocal{R}
. Based on the singular value decomposition of
\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}
, we can find two matrices
\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)}
and
\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}
with rank at most
\pazocal{R}
such that

\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}=\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}
and
\langle\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}\rangle=0
.
Then it holds
\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}/2
.
Hence,
for any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle=\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{M}},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\langle\boldsymbol{M}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\small{\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})}}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{M}},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(i)}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\small{\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})}}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{M}},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle.

Taking supremum with respect to
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})
on both sides of the last inequality, we accomplish the proof of this lemma.
∎

Proof of (
S46
):

Denote
S^{N-1}=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\mid\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1\}
, and let
\bar{S}^{N-1}
be a minimal
(1/4)
-net of
S^{N-1}
in the Euclidean norm. Fix
j\geq 1
. By Lemma 5.4 in
Vershynin, (2010)
,

\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq 2\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\bar{S}^{N-1}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{u}=2\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\bar{S}^{N-1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}.

Then for any
K>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\geq K\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\bar{S}^{N-1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}\geq K/2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 9^{N}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S^{N-1}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}\geq K/2\right\},

(S58)

where we used the fact that the cardinality of
\bar{S}^{N-1}
satisfies
|\bar{S}^{N-1}|\leq 9^{N}
. For any
\boldsymbol{u}\in S^{N-1}
, applying Lemma
S.10
(ii) with
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}
, and
T_{0}=1-p-j
, together with the result

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})=\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})

as implied by Lemma
S.11
(i), we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\{(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}\}\right|\geq\delta\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\right\}\leq 2e^{-c\min(\delta,\delta^{2})T}.

holds for any
\delta>0
. In addition, by Lemma
S.11
(i),

\mathbb{E}\{(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}\}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}).

In view of the above result and taking
\delta=j
, we further have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j})^{2}\geq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\leq 2e^{-cjT}.

(S59)

Combining (
S58
) and (
S59
), if
T\geq 2N\log 9/c
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\geq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\leq 2e^{-jN\log 9}.

(S60)

By considering the union bound over all
j\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists j\geq 1:\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\geq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2e^{-jN\log 9}\leq 3e^{-N\log 9}.

and hence (
S46
).

Proof of (
S47
):
We first fix
j\geq 1
.
Note that
