\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{M},\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\rangle
. Moreover, it can be verified that

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}.

Thus, by (
S60
) and Lemma
S.12
, for any
K>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq K\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{4},\,\Big\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\Big\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2e^{-jN\log 9}

\displaystyle\leq e^{9N\pazocal{R}}\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\pazocal{R})}\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{4},\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2e^{-jN\log 9}.

Then, applying Lemma
S.9
with
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}
, we have the pointwise bound for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{K}{4},\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{K^{2}T}{64\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}\right\}=\exp\{-9N\pazocal{R}(j+1)\},

if we choose

K=24\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)(j\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{2})N\pazocal{R}}{T}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq 24\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)(j\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{2})N\pazocal{R}}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq e^{-9jN\pazocal{R}}+2e^{-jN\log 9}\leq 3e^{-jN\log 9}.

Taking the union bound over all
j\geq 1
as in the proof of (
S46
) and noting that
(j\sigma^{2}+1)(j\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{2})\leq(2j\sigma^{2}+1)^{2}
, we can verify (
S47
).

S6.13
Proof of Lemma
S.8

The following covering result for low-Tucker-rank tensors is used in the proof of Lemma
S.8
.

Lemma S.13

(Covering number for low-Tucker-rank tensors)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}:\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\textup{F}}\leq 1,\textup{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)})\leq\pazocal{R}_{i},i=1,2\}
. For any
\epsilon>0
, let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\epsilon;\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
be a minimal
\epsilon
-net for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
in the Frobenius norm. Then the cardinality of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\epsilon;\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
satisfies

|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\epsilon;\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}p_{3}+p_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1}+p_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}}.

Proof of Lemma
S.13
.

The proof of this lemma is straightforward given the proof of Lemma 2 in
Rauhut et al., (2017)
.
∎

Proof of (i):
Notice that the results for
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}
in (
S17
) can be generalized to any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})
with
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(r+2s)}
, where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*})
. That is, by a method similar to that for (
S17
), we can show that for any
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(r+2s)}
,

\displaystyle 0.5(\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\varpi_{1}\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2})\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\varpi_{2}\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2},

(S61)

where

\varpi_{1}=\sqrt{2}c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\alpha\quad\text{and}\quad\varpi_{2}=\frac{\sqrt{2}\alpha}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}.

Thus, if
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1
, then
1\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq 2
and
\varpi_{2}^{-1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}\leq\varpi_{1}^{-1}
. As a result,

\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}\subset\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\mid\boldsymbol{a}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}\},

where

\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)}=\left\{\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s}\mid\varpi_{2}^{-1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}\leq\varpi_{1}^{-1}\right\}

and

\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times d}\mid\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(2\pazocal{R}_{1},2\pazocal{R}_{2}),1\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq 2\}.

Hence, the problem of covering
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
can be converted into that of covering
\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}
.

For any fixed
\epsilon>0
, let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)
be a minimal
\epsilon/(2\varpi_{2})
-net for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)}
in the Euclidean norm, and let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
be a minimal
\epsilon/2
-net for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}
in the Frobenius norm. Then denote

\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)=\left\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{a}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)\right\}.

Thus, for every
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
with
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
such that

\|\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon/(2\varpi_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\epsilon/2.

(S62)

Since
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})
, it follows from (
S61
) and (
S62
) that

\displaystyle\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\varpi_{2}\|\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.

In addition, note that
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Xi}
. Therefore,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is a generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.
Moreover, by a standard volumetric argument (see also Corollary 4.2.13 in
Vershynin, (2018)
for details) and Lemma
S.13
, the cardinalities of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
satisfy

\displaystyle\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)|\leq(r+2s)\log\{6\varpi_{2}/(\varpi_{1}\epsilon)\}\quad\text{and}\quad\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)|\leq\{4\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+2(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})N\}\log(18/\epsilon).

Noting that
\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}^{-1}\asymp 1
is independent of
\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)|\leq\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)|+\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)|\lesssim\left\{\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+(\pazocal{R}_{1}+\pazocal{R}_{2})N\right\}\log(1/\epsilon).

Proof of (ii):
Since

\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)}
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}
, we have

\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\left\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{a}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(1)},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}\right\}.

Thus, by (
S61
), for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
, it holds

c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}:=0.5(1+\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}^{-1})\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq 2+\varpi_{2}\varpi_{1}^{-1}:=C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}.

Since
\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}^{-1}=c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}\asymp 1
is independent of
\epsilon
, (ii) is proved.

Proof of (iii):

From the proof of (i), for every
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}:=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}:=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
such that
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(1)}(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
satisfy (
S62
). Since
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}^{(2)}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{(2)}
, we have
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(4\pazocal{R}_{1},4\pazocal{R}_{2})
.
Then by considering the higher-order singular value decomposition for
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}
, we can find four tensors
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(2\pazocal{R}_{1},2\pazocal{R}_{2})
with
1\leq i\leq 4
such that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i}
and
\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{j}\rangle=0
for all
i\neq j
.
As a result, we can show that

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}}{4},\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i},

where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\left((\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}})/4,\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i}\right)\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}
.
Moreover, by (
S61
), (
S62
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\left\{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\frac{\varpi_{2}}{4}\|\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\right\}\leq 2\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\varpi_{2}\|\boldsymbol{a}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\leq 1.5\epsilon.

Therefore, for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, we can show that

\displaystyle\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle=\langle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\langle(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}})_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\langle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i})_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\langle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\langle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+1.5\epsilon\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle,

Taking supremum over all
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
on both sides, we accomplish the proof of (
S48
).
The proof of (
S49
) follows the same arguments as those for (
S48
) except that the above inequalities are revised to

\displaystyle\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}-\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bar{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+1.5\epsilon\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

(S63)

The proof of Lemma
S.8
is complete.

S6.14
Proof of Lemma
S.9

Note that
\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}\rangle
, where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}
is mean-zero and
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian. Moreover, it holds
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Then by a straightforward multivariate generalization of Lemma 4.2 in
Simchowitz et al., (2018)
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\geq a,\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\rVert^{2}\leq b\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}\rangle\geq a,\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\rVert^{2}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})b\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})b}\right\}.

The proof is complete.

S6.15
Proof of Lemma
S.10

Proof of (i):

First it is obvious that
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series. Without loss of generality, we let
T_{0}=0
in what follows.

It is worth noting that under Assumption
2
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}
, and all coordinates of the vector
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{T-1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{T-2}^{\prime},\dots)^{\prime}
are independent and
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian with mean zero and variance one.
In addition, by the vector MA(
\infty
) representation of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
, we have
\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}=\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\boldsymbol{\xi}
,
where

\displaystyle\underset{TM\times\infty}{\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{3}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{T}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{T-1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
&&\ddots&&&&\\
&&&&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\end{matrix}\right).

(S64)

Then, it holds

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}^{\prime})=\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}.

(S65)

Observe that

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\prime}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\boldsymbol{\xi}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{\xi}
is a vector with independent, zero-mean and sub-Gaussian coordinates, we can apply the Hanson-Wright inequality
(
Vershynin,, 2018
)
to obtain that for any
\iota>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-T\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\iota\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-c\min\left(\frac{\iota}{\sigma^{2}\|(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}},\frac{\iota^{2}}{\sigma^{4}\|(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}\right)\right\}.

(S66)

Note that by (
S65
),

\|(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\|\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})
, and
\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}
is a
TM\times TM
matrix. Then

\|(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{TM}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}).

Taking
\iota=\sigma^{2}\sqrt{T}M\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})
in (
S66
), the proof of (i) is complete.

Proof of (ii):

Define the vector
\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=((\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{T})^{\prime},\dots,(\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{1})^{\prime})^{\prime}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}
. Then
\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}
, where
\boldsymbol{P}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}
. As a result,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}^{\prime}\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}
. Similar to (
S65
), it follows from the Hanson-Wright inequality that
for any
\iota>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-T\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\iota\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-c\min\left(\frac{\iota}{\sigma^{2}\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}},\frac{\iota^{2}}{\sigma^{4}\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}\right)\right\}.

(S67)

By (
S65
), we have

\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\|\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{M}^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\|\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\trace(\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P})=\trace(\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\trace\{(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M}^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=\textrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})^{\prime}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{TQ})\textrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\leq T\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2},

where the second equality follows from (
S65
). As a result,

\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\trace(\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{P})}\leq\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\trace(\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\prime})}\leq\sqrt{T}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}.

Taking
\iota=\delta\sigma^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}
in (
S67
), the proof of (ii) is complete.

S6.16
Proof of Lemma
S.11

Proof of (i):
Consider the spectral density of
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
,

\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)=(2\pi)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\theta\in[-\pi,\pi].

Let

\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})=\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta))\quad\text{and}\quad\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})=\min_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta))

Along the lines of
Basu and Michailidis, (2015)
, it holds

2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}),

2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}),

and

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*});

(S68)

see Proposition 2.3 therein.
Thus, (i) is proved.

Proof of (ii):
First, since
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
and
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
is stationary with mean zero, the time series
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
is also zero-mean and stationary, where
\mathscr{W}(B)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}B^{i}
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote by
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-\ell}^{\prime})
the lag-
\ell
covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}
, and then
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)e^{i\ell\theta}d\theta
.
For any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}^{\prime}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\right)\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(k-j)\boldsymbol{W}^{\prime}_{k}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)e^{-i(j-k)\theta}\boldsymbol{W}_{k}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta,

(S69)

where
\mathscr{W}(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}z^{j}
for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, and
\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})=\big\{\mathscr{W}(e^{i\theta})\big\}^{\prime}
is the conjugate transpose of
\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})
.
Since
\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)
is Hermitian,
\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}
is real for all
\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]
. Then it is easy to see that

\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\leq\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}.

Moreover, since
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{i\ell\theta}d\theta=0
for any
\ell\neq 0
, we can show that

\displaystyle\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}e^{-i(j-k)\theta}\boldsymbol{W}_{k}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=2\pi\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{W}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}.

which, together with the fact of
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
, implies that

\displaystyle 2\pi\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{W})\leq\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta\leq 2\pi\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{W}).

(S70)

In view of (
S68
)–(
S70
), we accomplish the proof of (
S50
).

To verify (
S51
), note that the spectral density of
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is

\boldsymbol{f}_{w}(\theta)=\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\theta\in[-\pi,\pi];

see Section 9.2 of
Priestley, (1981)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w})=\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w}(\theta))

\displaystyle\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}\{\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\}

\displaystyle=\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}e^{-ij\theta}\right\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\right)^{2}

In addition, by a method similar to the proof of Proposition 2.3 in
Basu and Michailidis, (2015)
, we can show that

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w}).

Combining the above results with (
S68
), the proof of (
S51
) is complete.

S6.17
Proof of Theorem
4

For a matrix
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
, we denote by
\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{0}
the number of nonzero elements in
\boldsymbol{X}
,
\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{2,0}
the number of nonzero rows in
\boldsymbol{X}
, and define
\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{1}=\|\vect(\boldsymbol{X})\|_{1}
and
\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{2,1}=\sum_{i=1}^{p_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{x}_{i}\|_{2}
, where
\boldsymbol{x}_{i}
’s are the row vectors of
\boldsymbol{X}
.
Let

\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times\infty}\mid\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{3}\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathbb{S}},\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega},\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathbb{S}}=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}\mid\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\pazocal{U}_{i},i=1\text{ or }2\}
. It is noteworthy that under the conditions of Theorem
4
,
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}:=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}
.
For simplicity, we further denote the perturbation of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}^{*}
by
\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
.
Moreover, we denote

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}\mid\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}\times\pazocal{R}_{2}\times d},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\pazocal{U}_{\mathrm{S},i},i=1,2\},

where
\pazocal{U}_{\mathrm{S},i}=\{\boldsymbol{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times\pazocal{R}_{i}}\mid\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}=\boldsymbol{I}_{\pazocal{R}_{i}},\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{0}\leq s_{i}\}
, and then define

\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\left\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})\right\},

(S71)

and
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})\cap\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\}
.

The proof of Theorem
4
depends directly on the following three lemmas.

Lemma S.14

(Strong convexity and smoothness properties for the sparse model)
.

Under Assumptions
2
–
6
, if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, then with probability at least
1-2e^{-c\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-3e^{-c\bar{s}_{2}\log N}
,

\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}},

where

\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bar{s}_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}(1+\log N\pazocal{R}_{i})
and

\bar{s}_{i}=(s_{i}+\underline{u}^{-1})\pazocal{R}_{i}
, with
i=1
or
2
.

Lemma S.15

(Deviation bound for the sparse model)
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
S.14
and if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, given that
\lambda\gtrsim\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{S}}/{T}}
,

\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle\right|\lesssim\lambda\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\right)/4+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}

holds with probability at least
1-3e^{-cd_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-4e^{-cs_{2}\log N(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2})}
, where
d_{\pazocal{S}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+\sum_{i=1}^{2}s_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}(1+\log N\pazocal{R}_{i})
and
\tau=\sqrt{(d+\log N)/T}
.

Lemma S.16

(Effects of initial values)
.

Under Assumptions
2
–
5
, if
T\gtrsim\bar{s}_{2}
, then with probability at least
1-c\sqrt{\bar{s}_{2}/T}(1+\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}/d_{\pazocal{S}}})
,

|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\lesssim\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}/4,\quad|S_{i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{S}}}{T}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}/4,\quad i=2,3,\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta),

where
\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}
and
d_{\pazocal{S}}
are defined in Lemmas
S.14
and
S.15
, and
\bar{s}_{i}=(s_{i}+\underline{u}^{-1})\pazocal{R}_{i}
for
i=1
or
2
.

Now we give the proof of Theorem
4
. Denote
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}
.
Note that
\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}
.
Due to the optimality of
\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}
, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\|_{1}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{1},

Then, since
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}+\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t})=\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
, it follows from (
S19
) and (
S20
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{3}S_{k}(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}})+\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\|_{1}\right),

(S72)

where
S_{k}(\cdot)
for
1\leq k\leq 3
are the initialization error terms defined as in (
S18
), and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{S}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}^{*}
,
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\omega}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}
, and
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}=\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}
for
i=1
or
2
.
On the right-hand side of (
S72
), denote the event that the first term are bounded by
\lambda(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1})
and
\tau\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}
as
\pazocal{I}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pazocal{I}_{1}=\left\{\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle\right|\lesssim\lambda\left(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\right)/4+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}\right\}.\end{split}

On event
\pazocal{I}_{1}
, if we multiply
2
to both sides of (
S72
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}&\leq\lambda\left(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}+2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{1}-2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\|_{1}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+2\sum_{k=1}^{3}S_{k}(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}})+4\tau\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}.\end{split}

Denote by
\mathbb{S}_{i}
the index set of the nonzero entries of
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}
, and by
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{C}
the complement of
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}
, for
i=1
or
2
. By the elementwise sparsity of each

\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}
in Assumption
4
, the cardinality of the index set
|\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}|=\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{0}\leq s_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}
for
i=1
or
2
.
Note that
\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\|_{1}\geq\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}^{C}}+(\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}-\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}=\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}^{C}}\|_{1}+\|(\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}-\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}
,
(\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}=\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}
and
\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\leq\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}+\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}^{C}}\|_{1}
, so we have

\begin{split}\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}&\leq\lambda(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2})+3\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}-\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}^{C}}\|_{1}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+2\sum_{k=1}^{3}S_{k}(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}})+4\tau\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}.\end{split}

Next, we assume that there is a lower bound for
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
and then define the event
\pazocal{I}_{2}=\{T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\kappa_{1}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\}
, where
\kappa_{1}=\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\min\{1,c_{\bar{\rho}}^{2}\}
and
c_{\bar{\rho}}
is an absolute constant defined in Lemma
S.2
in the Appendix. Moreover, we assume that the initialization error terms have a upper bound, and denote the event

\pazocal{I}_{3}=\{S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}})\lesssim\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}/4,S_{k}(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}})\lesssim\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}/4,k=2\text{ or }3\}.

By Assumptions
4
and
5
, let
\bar{s}_{i}=(s_{i}+\underline{u}^{-1})\pazocal{R}_{i}
, and then
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}
has an elementwise sparsity of at most
\bar{s}_{i}
for
i=1
or
2
.
Hence, by the perturbation bounds in Lemma
S.17
and Lemma
1
,

\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\bar{s}_{i}}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq c_{\Delta}^{-1}{\beta}^{-1}{C\eta_{i}}\sqrt{\bar{s}_{i}}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

Suppose that
c_{\Delta}^{-2}{\beta}^{-2}{C^{2}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}\tau\lesssim\kappa_{1}/8
, on the events
\pazocal{I}_{1},\pazocal{I}_{2}
and
\pazocal{I}_{3}
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\lambda(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2})+3\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}})_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\lambda(\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2})+3\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sqrt{s_{i}}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},

where by the perturbation bounds in Lemma
S.17
and Lemma
1
,
\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq c_{\Delta}^{-1}{\beta}^{-1}{C\eta_{i}}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
.
Similarly, we can show

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\omega}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(Lemma \ref{lemma:sparse_perturb_svd})}}{\leq}{\beta}^{-1}{C(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\omega}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(Lemma \ref{lemma:delnorm})}}{\leq}c_{\Delta}^{-1}\max\left({\beta}^{-1}{C(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})},1\right)\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},

Since
\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}\leq 2\sqrt{x+y}
for any
x,y\geq 0
, we have

\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim c_{\Delta}^{-1}{\beta}^{-1}{(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})}\sqrt{s_{1}+s_{2}}\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

And the estimation error bound and in-sample prediction error bound are given by

\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\Delta}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\lesssim(c_{\Delta}{\beta}\kappa_{1})^{-1}{(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})}\sqrt{s_{1}+s_{2}}\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{x}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim(c_{\Delta}^{2}{\beta}^{2}\kappa_{1})^{-1}(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})^{2}(s_{1}+s_{2})\lambda^{2},

respectively.

In the second part, we show the conditions that events
\pazocal{I}_{1},\pazocal{I}_{2}
and
\pazocal{I}_{3}
occur with high probability.
First, denote by
d_{\pazocal{S}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+\sum_{i=1}^{2}s_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}(1+\log N\pazocal{R}_{i})
the sample complexity of the model. If
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, given that
\lambda\gtrsim\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{S}}/{T}}
and
\tau=\sqrt{(d+\log N)/T}
, it follows from Lemma
S.15
that the event
\pazocal{I}_{1}
holds with probability at least
1-3e^{-cd_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-4e^{-cs_{2}\log N(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2})}
. Moreover, if
T\gtrsim\beta^{-4}\eta_{1}^{-2}\eta_{2}^{-2}\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}(d+\log N)
, the condition that
c_{\Delta}^{-2}{\beta}^{-2}{C^{2}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}\tau\lesssim\kappa_{1}/8
holds.

Secondly, if
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, it follows from Lemma
S.14
that the event
\pazocal{I}_{2}
holds with probability at least
1-2e^{-c\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}-3e^{-c\bar{s}_{2}\log N}
, where
\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bar{s}_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}(1+\log N\pazocal{R}_{i})
.

Finally, if
T\gtrsim\bar{s}_{2}
, then it follows from Lemma
S.16
that with probability at least
1-c\sqrt{\bar{s}_{2}/T}(1+\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}/d_{\pazocal{S}}})
, the event
\pazocal{I}_{3}
holds.

S6.18
Proof of Lemma
S.14

The proof of this lemma is largely based on some existing results in the proof of Lemma
S.3
in Section
S6.7
.
First of all, under Assumptions
4
&
5
, there are at most

\underline{u}^{-1}\pazocal{R}_{i}
nonzero rows in
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
and
s_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}
ones in
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}
, for all
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\pazocal{U}_{i}
with
i=1
or
2
. Hence, for any
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}
, we only need to consider those that satisfy

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S}}(\bar{s}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1},\bar{s}_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2},2\pazocal{R}_{1},2\pazocal{R}_{2})
and
\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}(\bar{s}_{1}(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}),\bar{s}_{2}(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}),2(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}))
for all
j\geq 1
.

Then, it remains to show that the following two results hold for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
that satisfy the above sparsity conditions.

(i)

If
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta):\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{1}}{8}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\delta^{2}\kappa_{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-c\bar{d}_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

(ii)

If
T\gtrsim\bar{s}_{2}\log N
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\right\}\geq 1-3e^{-c\bar{s}_{2}\log N}.

The result in (i) can be jointly obtained from (
S28
), (
S30
) and Lemma
S.20
. While to obtain the result in (ii), we first denote by
\mathbb{K}(s)=\{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{0}\leq s,\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq 1\}
the set of
s
-sparse vectors. Note that for all
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\trace\left\{\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{R}_{j}^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{K}(\bar{s}_{2})}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{v}\right).

Combining this with (
S32
) & (
S34
) and (
S83
), if
T\gtrsim\bar{s}_{2}\log N
, with probability at least
1-3e^{-\bar{s}_{2}\log N\log 9}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{R}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{j\sigma^{2}+1}\right)^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}),

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{j\sigma^{2}+1}\asymp 1
. Thus (ii) is verified. The rest of the proof is same as the proof of Lemma
S.3
and hence is omitted here.

S6.19
Proof of Lemma
S.15

The proof of this lemma closely follows the proof of Lemma
S.4
. Essentially, we only need to show the following two intermediate results.

(i)

If
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|\leq\lambda\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\right)/4+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\geq 1-e^{-cd_{\pazocal{S}}}-2e^{-cd_{\pazocal{S}}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.

(ii)

If
T\gtrsim(s_{1}+s_{2})(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}+\log N(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2}))
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}}\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\right|\leq\lambda\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}\|_{1}\right)/4+\tau\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\geq 1-4e^{-s_{2}\log N(\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2})\log 9}.

Proof of (i):

Note that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*}
, we then have
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}}=\stk(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{i}
, where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1}&=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1,i,m},\quad\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2,i,m},\quad\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,j,m,h},\hskip 5.69054pt\text{and}\\
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{4}&=\stk(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{D}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*}),\end{split}

where for any
\boldsymbol{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{r+2s})^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times d}
, the bilinear functional
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})
is defined as in (
S3
), and moreover, for
1\leq i\leq N
, the
N\times N\times(d+r+2s)
tensors
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1,i}
,
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2,i}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,m}
are defined respectively by

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1,i,m}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{\bar{e}}_{m}^{\prime})\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}),\quad\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2,i,m}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*\prime}\times_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{e}}_{m}^{\prime})),

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69054pt\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,j,m,h}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{\bar{e}}_{m}^{\prime})\times_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(j,h)}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{e}}_{h}^{\prime}))

where
\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell},\boldsymbol{\bar{e}}_{\ell}
and
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{e}}_{\ell}
are coordinate vectors whose
\ell
-th element is
1
and the others are
0
of dimensional
N,\pazocal{R}_{1}
and
\pazocal{R}_{2}
respectively, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}}^{(k,\ell)}
is the
(k,\ell)
-th element of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{i}},i=1,2
with
1\leq\ell\leq N
.
Since
\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=1
and
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}_{(i)}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}
for
i=1
or
2
, by Assumption
3
, the norms of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{k}
’s further satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}_{(1)}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}|\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}|,\quad\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}_{(2)}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}|\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}|,\\
&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,j,m,h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}_{(1)}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}||\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(j,h)}|\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}||\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(j,h)}|,\hskip 5.69054pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69054pt\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{4}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\Delta}\left(\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}+\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right),\end{split}

(S73)

where the norm bound on
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{4}
follows from Lemma
1
.

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{\rm{stack}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{k}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{k,i,m})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{4})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,j,m,h})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{1,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(1,s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2},1,\pazocal{R}_{2})}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{2,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1},1,\pazocal{R}_{1},1)}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{3,i,j,m,h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(1,1,1,1)}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{4}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1},s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\rangle.

Combine this with (
S73
) and (
S82
) in Lemma
S.20
, we can show (i).

Proof of (ii):

Note that
\boldsymbol{R}_{j}=\boldsymbol{R}_{1j}+\boldsymbol{R}_{2j}+\boldsymbol{R}_{3j}
, where
\boldsymbol{R}_{kj},1\leq k\leq 3
are defined in (
S9
). In
\boldsymbol{R}_{1j}
and
\boldsymbol{R}_{2j}
, we have
\boldsymbol{G}_{k}-\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{*}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\boldsymbol{S}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}+\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\boldsymbol{S}_{k}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}\boldsymbol{S}_{k}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{S}_{k}-\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}
for all
\boldsymbol{G}_{k}
-matrices, while in
\boldsymbol{R}_{3j}
, we have
\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{*}=\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{*}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}
for all
\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{*}
-matrices. It is then possible to further break down
\boldsymbol{R}_{1j}
into
\boldsymbol{M}_{1j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{3j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{5j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{7j}
and
\boldsymbol{R}_{2j}
into
\boldsymbol{M}_{2j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{4j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{6j}+\boldsymbol{M}_{8j}
, respectively, where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}&=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m},\hskip 5.69054pt1\leq k\leq 2,\hskip 5.69054pt\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m},\hskip 5.69054pt3\leq k\leq 4,\\
\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}&=\sum_{i,\ell=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,\ell,m,h},\hskip 5.69054pt5\leq k\leq 6,\\
\boldsymbol{M}_{7j}&=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\nabla\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{k}^{*})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I}-\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I*})\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\sum_{h=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h}-\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h*})\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime},\\
\boldsymbol{M}_{8j}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{I}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{k})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})^{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I}-\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I*})\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{s}\sum_{h=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{k})(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h}-\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h*})\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*\prime},\end{split}

where
\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{\bar{e}}_{m}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{T}_{kj}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{*}
for
1\leq k\leq 2
,
\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}^{*}\boldsymbol{T}_{(k-2)j}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{e}}_{m}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\prime}
for
3\leq k\leq 4
and
\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,\ell,m,h}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(\ell,h)}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{\bar{e}}_{m}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{T}_{(k-4)j}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{e}}_{h}\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}^{\prime}
for
5\leq k\leq 6
are all
N\times N
matrices, with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{T}_{1j}&=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\nabla\ell_{j}^{I}(\lambda_{k}^{*})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I}+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\sum_{h=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h}\hskip 5.69054pt\text{and}\\
\boldsymbol{T}_{2j}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{I}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{k})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})^{2}\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{I}+\sum_{k=1}^{s}\sum_{h=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\ell_{j}^{II,h}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{k})(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})\boldsymbol{S}_{k}^{II,h}.\end{split}

(S74)

For the tidiness of notation, we further let
\boldsymbol{M}_{9j}=\boldsymbol{R}_{3j}
, and then
\boldsymbol{R}_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{9}\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}
. Note that the norms of
\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}
’s further satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1j,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}C_{L}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}|,\hskip 54.06023pt\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2j,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{L}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}|,\\
&\|\boldsymbol{M}_{3j,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}C_{L}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}|,\hskip 54.06023pt\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4j,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{L}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(i,m)}|,\\
&\|\boldsymbol{M}_{5j,i,\ell,m,h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}C_{L}\bar{\rho}^{j}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}||\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(\ell,h)}|,\hskip 5.69054pt\|\boldsymbol{M}_{6j,i,\ell,m,h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{L}\bar{\rho}^{j}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{1}}^{(i,m)}||\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{2}}^{(\ell,h)}|,\\
&\|\boldsymbol{M}_{7j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{2}C_{L}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}},\hskip 22.76219pt\|\boldsymbol{M}_{8j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{L}\bar{\rho}^{j}\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2}\delta_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}},\hskip 11.38109pt\text{and}\hskip 11.38109pt\|\boldsymbol{M}_{9j}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{L}C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{G}$}}\bar{\rho}^{j}\alpha\delta_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{2},\end{split}

(S75)

As a result,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{R}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\right|&=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle+\sum_{k=3}^{4}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=5}^{6}\sum_{i,\ell=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,\ell,m,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle+\sum_{k=7}^{9}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\\
&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(1,s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2},1)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=3}^{4}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,m}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1},1,1)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=5}^{6}\sum_{i,\ell=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}}\sum_{h=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{kj,i,\ell,m,h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(1,1,1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=7}^{9}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{kj}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1}\pazocal{R}_{1},s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1}\wedge\pazocal{R}_{2})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\rangle\end{split}

(S76)

Then by (
S75
), (
S76
) and (
S84
) in Lemma
S.22
, (ii) is verified.

S6.20
Proof of Lemma
S.16

The proof of this lemma follows closely from the proof of Lemma
S.5
. The main difference lies in the sparsity conditions. Specifically, denote the index sets of the nonzero rows and columns in
\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}
by
\mathbb{S}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}_{2}
, and those in
\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{j}
by
\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{1}
and
\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{2}
, respectively. By Assumptions
4
and
5
, it holds that the cardinality of
\mathbb{S}_{i}
and
\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{i}
satisfy
|\mathbb{S}_{i}|\leq s_{i}\pazocal{R}_{i}
and
|\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{i}|\leq\bar{s}_{i}
for
i=1
or
2
.
Then, note that
\mathbb{E}(\|(\boldsymbol{y}_{t})_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2})\leq s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})
holds by Lemma
S.11
, and thus for all
j\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2})\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}^{2})\right\}^{1/2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\mathbb{E}(\|(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}\leq C_{*}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})s_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}}

(S77)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}\right)&\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}^{1/2}\\
&\leq\delta C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\bar{s}_{2}}.\end{split}

(S78)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
S78
),

\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{j+k}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\bar{s}_{2}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{2}\bar{s}_{2}}{T},

where
C_{2}=2C_{1}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{3}\asymp 1
. Similarly, by (
S77
) and (
S78
),

\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\delta C_{*}C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j+k}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\sqrt{s_{2}\bar{s}_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}}\leq\frac{\delta C_{3}\kappa_{2}\sqrt{s_{2}\bar{s}_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}}}{T},

where
C_{3}=2C_{*}C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{2}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{3}\asymp 1
. Moreover, note that
\mathbb{E}(\|(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{1}}\|_{2})\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\|(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{1}}\|_{2}^{2})}\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\bar{s}_{1}}
. Then by (
S78
) and a method similar to the above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}\delta C_{1}\bar{\rho}^{j}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})}\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta C_{4}\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})}\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}}{T},

where
C_{4}=2C_{1}\bar{\rho}/(1-\bar{\rho})^{2}\asymp 1
.
By Markov’s inequality, we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{1}\right\}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\}}{\delta^{2}C_{2}\kappa_{1}}\leq\frac{\kappa_{2}\bar{s}_{2}}{\kappa_{1}T}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\bar{s}_{2}}{T}},

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{S}}}{T}}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}s_{2}\bar{s}_{2}\pazocal{R}_{2}}{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})Td_{\pazocal{S}}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\bar{s}_{2}}{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})T}},

and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)}}|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\delta C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{\pazocal{S}}}{T}}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}{Td_{\pazocal{S}}}},

where the last inequality in (
S43
) uses the condition that
T\gtrsim\bar{s}_{2}
.
Then the sum of the above three tail probabilities is
(1+\sqrt{\kappa_{2}/\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})})\sqrt{\bar{s}_{2}/T}(1+\sqrt{\bar{s}_{1}/d_{\pazocal{S}}})
.

S6.21
Auxiliary lemmas for the proofs of Lemmas
S.14
and
S.15

The proofs of Lemmas
S.14
and
S.15
rely on the following auxiliary results.

Lemma S.17

(HOSVD perturbation bound)
.

Suppose that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times\boldsymbol{U}_{1}\times\boldsymbol{U}_{2}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{S}}$}\times\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{1}\times\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{2}
are two HOSVD for
\mathscr{G}
and
\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}
, with the same multilinear ranks
(\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
along the first and second modes. Under Assumptions
5
and
6
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{S}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{C(\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})}{\beta}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\\
\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{C\eta_{i}}{\beta}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},\end{split}

(S79)

where
\eta_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{\pazocal{R}_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{2}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)})/\sigma_{j}^{2}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)})
for
i=1
or
2
. Moreover,

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{S}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{S}$}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widetilde{U}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

(S80)

Proof of Lemma
S.17
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma 1 in
Wang et al., (2022)
.
∎

Lemma S.18

(Covering number for sparse-and-low-Tucker-rank tensors)
.

Let

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{U}_{1}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}:\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{\textup{F}}\leq 1,\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pazocal{R}_{1}\times\pazocal{R}_{2}\times d},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\pazocal{U}_{\mathrm{S},i},i=1,2\},

where
\pazocal{U}_{\mathrm{S},i}=\{\boldsymbol{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times\pazocal{R}_{i}}\mid\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}=\boldsymbol{I}_{\pazocal{R}_{i}},\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{0}\leq s_{i}\}
. For any
\epsilon>0
, let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
be a minimal
\epsilon
-net for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
in the Frobenius norm. Then
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
has cardinality satisfying

|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})|\leq\binom{N\pazocal{R}_{1}}{s_{1}}\binom{N\pazocal{R}_{2}}{s_{2}}\left(\frac{9}{\epsilon}\right)^{\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+s_{1}+s_{2}}.

Proof of Lemma
S.18
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma
S.13
.
∎

Recall from (
S71
) that

\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\left\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}(\boldsymbol{a},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{B}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})\right\},

and
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})=\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})\cap\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N\times(d+r+2s)}\mid\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\}
.

Lemma S.19

(Covering number and discretization for
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}
)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<2/3
, let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
be a minimal generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
. In (ii) – (iii), denote
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
and
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
by
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}
, respectively.

(i)

The cardinality of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
satisfies

\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\epsilon;s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})|\lesssim(\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+s_{1}+s_{2})\log(1/\epsilon)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}s_{i}\log N\pazocal{R}_{i},

(ii)

There exist absolute constants
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}},C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}>0
such that for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})
, it holds
c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\leq\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}
.

(iii)

For any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(d+r+2s)}
and
\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(d+r+2s)\times T}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(1-1.5\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-1.5\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

Proof of Lemma
S.19
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma
S.18
and the proof of Lemma
S.8
.
∎

Lemma S.20
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2
and
3
hold and
T\gtrsim(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})^{2}d_{1}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})
. Let
\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}
be defined as in (
S25
). Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{c_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{1}}{8}\leq\inf_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sup_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 6C_{\mbox{\boldmath\tiny$\mathscr{M}$}}\kappa_{2}\right)\\
&\hskip 156.49014pt\geq 1-2e^{-cd_{1}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})}.\end{split}

(S81)

and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\small{\mbox{\boldmath\scriptsize$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R}_{1},\pazocal{R}_{2})}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})d_{1}}{T}}\right\}\geq 1-e^{-cd_{1}}-2e^{-cd_{1}\log(\kappa_{2}/\kappa_{1})},

(S82)

where
d_{1}=\pazocal{R}_{1}\pazocal{R}_{2}d+\sum_{i=1}^{2}s_{i}(1+\log N\pazocal{R}_{i})
.

Proof of Lemma
S.20
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma
S.19
and the proof of Lemma
S.6
.
∎

Lemma S.21

(Covering number and discretization for sparse low-rank matrices)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})=\{\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{U}_{1}\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{U}_{2}^{\prime}\mid\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1,\boldsymbol{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pazocal{R}\times\pazocal{R}},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times\pazocal{R}},\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{0}\leq s_{i},i=1\textrm{ or }2\}
, and let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})
be a minimal
1/2
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})
in the Frobenius norm. Then the cardinality of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})
satisfies

\log|\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})|\leq[\pazocal{R}+(s_{1}+s_{2})(1+\log N\pazocal{R})]\log 18.

Moreover, for any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
, it holds

\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S},1}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle\leq 4\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})}\langle\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle.

Proof of Lemma
S.21
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma
S.12
.
∎

Lemma S.22
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2
and
3
hold.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{K}(s)=\{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{0}\leq s,\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq 1\}
the set of
s
-sparse vectors.
If
T\gtrsim s\log N
, then

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall j\geq 1:\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{K}(s)}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{v}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\geq 1-3e^{-s\log N\log 9}.\end{split}

(S83)

(ii)

If
T\gtrsim(s_{1}+s_{2})(\pazocal{R}+\log N\pazocal{R})
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\forall j\geq 1:\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{1},s_{2},\pazocal{R})}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-j},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\rangle\leq 24\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})(2j\sigma^{2}+1)\\
&\hskip 79.66771pt\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})[\pazocal{R}+(s_{1}+s_{2})(1+\log N\pazocal{R})]}{T}}\Bigg\}\geq 1-4e^{-s_{2}\log N\pazocal{R}\log 9}.\end{split}

(S84)

Proof of Lemma
S.22
.

The proof of this lemma follows trivially from Lemma
S.21
, Lemma F.2 in
Basu and Michailidis, (2015)
and the proof of Lemma
S.7
.
∎
